# Luma AMD Speedrun: MXFP4 Kernel Optimization Plan

Created: 2026-03-11
Status: PENDING
Approved: No
Iterations: 0
Worktree: Yes

> **Status Lifecycle:** PENDING -> COMPLETE -> VERIFIED
> **Iterations:** Tracks implement->verify cycles
> **Deadline:** March 30, 2026 (19 days remaining)

## Summary

**Goal:** Optimize three GPU kernels (MXFP4 GEMM, MLA Decode, MXFP4 MoE) for AMD MI355X to beat the aiter reference baselines in the Luma AMD x GPU MODE Hackathon Phase 1. Priority order: GEMM -> MLA -> MoE.

**Architecture:** Each kernel lives in `research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/<name>/submission.py` and must implement `custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t`. Submissions are evaluated remotely on MI355X via Popcorn CLI. Scoring uses geometric mean of benchmark runtimes.

**Tech Stack:** Python, PyTorch, Triton (AMD ROCm backend), aiter library (AMD's optimized kernels), Popcorn CLI for remote submission.

## Scope

### In Scope

- Complete Phase 2 knowledge accumulation (GPU MODE lectures, aiter source study)
- Run baseline benchmarks via Popcorn CLI for all three kernels
- Optimize MXFP4 GEMM submission (`kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`)
- Optimize MLA Decode submission (`kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py`)
- Optimize MXFP4 MoE submission (`kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`)
- Leaderboard submissions for all three

### Out of Scope

- Phase 2 hackathon work (end-to-end DeepSeek-R1 optimization)
- Custom HIP C++ kernels (starting with Triton; HIP only if Triton can't reach targets)
- Local MI355X testing (hardware not available; all testing via Popcorn CLI)
- Changes to reference.py, task.py, eval.py, or utils.py (competition-provided files)

## Prerequisites

- Popcorn CLI installed and authenticated (DONE: GitHub manderson240)
- aiter library available in Popcorn remote environment (pre-installed)
- GPU MODE Discord access for community insights and lecture materials

## Context for Implementer

> **This is a GPU kernel optimization competition, not typical software engineering.**

- **Pattern:** Each kernel has the same structure: `submission.py` exports `custom_kernel(data) -> output`. The eval harness calls `generate_input()` from `reference.py`, passes data to your kernel, checks correctness via `check_implementation()`, then benchmarks.
- **Correctness tolerances:** GEMM: rtol=1e-2, atol=1e-2. MLA: rtol=1e-2, atol=1e-2. MoE: rtol=5e-2, atol=5e-2.
- **Current submissions are NOT optimized** — GEMM and MoE just call the same aiter functions as reference (1.0x speedup = no improvement). MLA has a naive torch implementation (SLOWER than reference).
- **No local testing:** All benchmarking happens on remote MI355X via `popcorn submit`. Iteration cycles are slow.
- **Key aiter APIs:** `aiter.gemm_a4w4`, `aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)`, `aiter.fused_moe`, `aiter.mla.mla_decode_fwd`
- **Gotcha:** The benchmark clears L2 cache between runs (`clear_l2_cache_large()`), so cache warming tricks won't help.
- **Gotcha:** Benchmark includes quantization time for GEMM (quantizing A to MXFP4 is part of timed region).

### Competition Rules (MUST FOLLOW)

1. All submissions via **Popcorn CLI** only
2. Scoring: **geometric mean** of benchmark runtimes (lower = better)
3. Must **beat the baseline** to receive points
4. **Top 10 fastest** kernels per problem considered for aggregate score
5. All code must be **original work**
6. If advancing to Phase 2, code must be **mergeable into AMD repos** (ATOM/vLLM/SGLang)
7. Only the **top scoring kernel** per problem from a team is considered

### Task 2: MXFP4 GEMM - Optimized Quantization

**Objective:** Optimize the MXFP4 GEMM by improving the A-matrix quantization step, which is included in the timed region. The current submission calls `aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)` for quantization + `aiter.gemm_a4w4` for GEMM. Optimize the quantization kernel.

**Dependencies:** Task 1

**Files:**
- Modify: `research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`

**Key Decisions / Notes:**
- The benchmark times the ENTIRE `custom_kernel()` call, including quantization of A
- Current flow: `quant_func(A, shuffle=True)` -> `gemm_a4w4(A_q, B_shuffle, ...)`
- Optimization path 1: Write a custom Triton quantization kernel optimized for the specific shapes (M=4-256, K=512-7168)
- Optimization path 2: Fuse quantization with GEMM (avoid writing quantized A to global memory)
- Optimization path 3: Use aiter's alternative Triton GEMM implementation (`aiter.ops.triton.gemm.basic.gemm_afp4wfp4`) as noted in reference.py:97
- Input shapes from benchmarks: M in {4,16,32,64,256}, N in {2112,2880,3072,4096,7168}, K in {512,1536,2048,7168}
- Small M values (4-64) are common — these are memory-bound, not compute-bound

**Definition of Done:**
- [ ] Quantization step is faster than `get_triton_quant` for benchmark shapes
- [ ] `popcorn submit --mode test` passes (correctness: rtol=1e-2, atol=1e-2)
- [ ] `popcorn submit --mode benchmark` shows improvement over baseline

**Verify:**
- `popcorn submit --mode test` for mxfp4-mm kernel passes all 4 test cases
- `popcorn submit --mode benchmark` shows geometric mean improvement

### Task 3: MXFP4 GEMM - Custom Triton GEMM Kernel

**Objective:** Write a custom Triton GEMM kernel for MXFP4 that is optimized for the specific shapes in the benchmark suite. Focus on small-M scenarios (M=4-64) where the workload is memory-bound.

**Dependencies:** Task 2

**Files:**
- Modify: `research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mxfp4-mm/submission.py`

**Key Decisions / Notes:**
- For small M (4-64), the GEMM is memory-bound: focus on maximizing memory bandwidth utilization
- For larger M (256), GEMM is compute-bound: focus on MFMA (Matrix Fused Multiply-Add) utilization
- Key technique: Fuse quantization + GEMM into a single kernel launch (saves one global memory write + read)
- Triton on ROCm: Use `tl.load`, `tl.store`, `tl.dot` — compiler handles MFMA mapping
- MXFP4 dequantization in Triton: load fp4x2 packed data, unpack to fp16/bf16 using bitwise ops, apply E8M0 scales
- Tile sizes to explore: BLOCK_M in {16,32,64,128}, BLOCK_N in {64,128,256}, BLOCK_K in {32,64,128}
- Consider split-K strategy for small M values
- Must handle the (16,16) shuffle layout that aiter expects, or operate on raw quantized data

**Definition of Done:**
- [ ] Custom Triton GEMM kernel implemented for MXFP4
- [ ] Handles all benchmark shapes correctly (M divisible by 64, K divisible by 64)
- [ ] `popcorn submit --mode test` passes all 4 test cases
- [ ] `popcorn submit --mode benchmark` beats aiter baseline on geometric mean

**Verify:**
- `popcorn submit --mode test` passes
- `popcorn submit --mode benchmark` shows improvement
- `popcorn submit --mode leaderboard` for official ranking

---

### Task 4: MLA Decode - MXFP4 KV Cache Attention

**Objective:** Implement an optimized MLA decode kernel that uses the MXFP4 KV cache (4x bandwidth savings over bf16, 2x over fp8) with fused dequantization.

**Dependencies:** Task 1

**Files:**
- Modify: `research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/mixed-mla/submission.py`

**Key Decisions / Notes:**
- **Big opportunity:** Reference uses fp8 KV cache. Using MXFP4 KV cache gives 2x bandwidth savings over reference.
- Current submission has bf16 and fp8 paths. Need to add MXFP4 path.
- MLA specifics: 1 KV head broadcast across 16-32 query heads (MQA). This is extremely memory-bound.
- KV buffer: full 576 dims for keys, first 512 dims for values
- Approach A: Dequantize MXFP4 to bf16/fp16 in shared memory, then compute attention normally
- Approach B: Keep computation in low precision (harder, requires careful accumulation)
- Variable-length batching via indptr — must handle per-request segment boundaries
- q_seq_len is typically 1 or 4 (decode mode), kv_seq_len up to 8192
- Consider using aiter's `mla_decode_fwd` with MXFP4 if it supports that dtype
- Alternative: Write custom Triton flash-attention-style kernel with MXFP4 dequant fused into K/V loading
- Split-KV strategy: Process KV in chunks to fit in shared memory, reduce across chunks

**Definition of Done:**
- [ ] MXFP4 KV cache attention kernel implemented
- [ ] Dequantization fused into attention (no separate bf16 materialization)
- [ ] `popcorn submit --mode test` passes all 6 test cases (rtol=1e-2, atol=1e-2)
- [ ] `popcorn submit --mode benchmark` beats fp8 reference on geometric mean

**Verify:**
- `popcorn submit --mode test` for mixed-mla kernel passes
- `popcorn submit --mode benchmark` shows improvement
- `popcorn submit --mode leaderboard` for official ranking

---

### Task 5: MoE - Optimized Dispatch & Fusion

**Objective:** Optimize the MXFP4 MoE kernel beyond aiter's `fused_moe`. Focus on activation quantization fusion and expert dispatch optimization.

**Dependencies:** Task 1

**Files:**
- Modify: `research/challenges/luma_amd_speedrun/kernels/moe-mxfp4/submission.py`

**Key Decisions / Notes:**
- Current submission is identical to reference (`fused_moe` call). Must do something different.
- aiter `fused_moe` already fuses: quant + stage1 GEMM + SwiGLU + stage2 GEMM + weighted reduction
- This is the HARDEST kernel to beat because aiter already does heavy fusion
- Possible optimizations:
  1. **Shared expert specialization:** Shared expert (always weight=1.0) could use a separate dense GEMM path optimized for all-token computation, instead of going through the sparse MoE dispatch
  2. **Better expert load balancing:** Custom token-to-expert mapping that improves GPU occupancy
  3. **Custom Triton MoE:** Write the full 2-stage pipeline in Triton with AMD-specific tuning
  4. **Parameter tuning:** aiter `fused_moe` may have tuneable parameters not exposed in the simple API
- DeepSeek-R1 shapes: d_hidden=7168, d_expert=256-2048, E=33-257, top_k=9
- The wide range of expert counts (33 vs 257) means different strategies may be optimal per config

**Definition of Done:**
- [ ] MoE kernel optimized beyond vanilla `fused_moe` call
- [ ] `popcorn submit --mode test` passes all 3 test cases (rtol=5e-2, atol=5e-2)
- [ ] `popcorn submit --mode benchmark` beats aiter baseline on geometric mean

**Verify:**
- `popcorn submit --mode test` for moe-mxfp4 kernel passes
- `popcorn submit --mode benchmark` shows improvement
- `popcorn submit --mode leaderboard` for official ranking

## Testing Strategy

- **Correctness:** `popcorn submit --mode test` (remote MI355X, checks against reference with tolerance)
- **Performance:** `popcorn submit --mode benchmark` (remote MI355X, geometric mean of runtimes)
- **Local validation:** Limited — can verify Python syntax and basic logic on Radeon 8060S, but MXFP4/aiter requires MI355X
- **Iteration strategy:** Write kernel locally -> submit via popcorn -> analyze results -> iterate

## Risks and Mitigations

| Risk | Likelihood | Impact | Mitigation |
|------|-----------|--------|------------|
| Can't beat aiter baseline (already highly optimized) | Medium | High | Focus on MXFP4 KV cache for MLA (new capability, not just optimization). For GEMM, optimize quantization step. For MoE, try shared expert specialization. |
| Slow iteration (remote-only testing) | High | Medium | Write thorough local tests for correctness (using torch on CPU/Radeon). Only submit to MI355X for performance. Batch test+benchmark in single submission. |
| Triton on ROCm has bugs/limitations | Medium | Medium | Fall back to aiter API with different parameters. Check GPU MODE Discord for known issues. |
| MXFP4 dequant in Triton is complex | Medium | Medium | Study aiter's `fp4_utils.py` for reference dequant logic. Start with simple dequant, optimize later. |
| Deadline pressure (19 days, learning phase needed) | High | High | GEMM first (smallest scope). Get one leaderboard submission ASAP, then iterate. |
| Correctness failures at tolerance boundary | Low | High | Test with tighter tolerances locally. Use float32 accumulation for all reductions. |

## Open Questions

- Does aiter's `mla_decode_fwd` support MXFP4 KV dtype natively, or only fp8/bf16?
- What Triton version is available in the Popcorn remote environment?
- Are there undocumented aiter parameters in `fused_moe` that could improve performance?
- What tile sizes does the MI355X MFMA instruction support for fp4?

### Deferred Ideas

- Custom HIP C++ kernels (only if Triton can't reach competitive performance)
- Assembly-level optimization of MFMA instruction scheduling
- Autotuning framework for Triton tile sizes across all benchmark shapes

     1→"""
     2→MLA (Multi-head Latent Attention) decode kernel — submission template.
     3→
     4→Implement custom_kernel() to beat the aiter a8w8 reference (fp8 Q + fp8 KV).
     5→
     6→DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb MLA config:
     7→  total_num_heads  = 128    (query heads before TP split)
     8→  num_heads        = 128 // tp  (query heads per device, tp=4 → 32, tp=8 → 16)
     9→  num_kv_heads     = 1      (shared latent KV head)
    10→  kv_lora_rank     = 512    (latent dim)
    11→  qk_rope_head_dim = 64     (RoPE dim)
    12→  qk_head_dim      = 576    (kv_lora_rank + qk_rope_head_dim, absorbed q/k dim)
    13→  v_head_dim       = 512    (= kv_lora_rank, output dim)
    14→  sm_scale         = 1/sqrt(576)
    15→
    16→KV buffer format (forward_absorb):
    17→  - Full 576 dims used as keys (for Q@K^T score computation)
    18→  - First 512 dims (kv_lora_rank) used as values (for output computation)
    19→
    20→Input tuple:
    21→  q:          (total_q, num_heads, 576) bfloat16 — absorbed query
    22→  kv_data:    dict with three KV cache formats:
    23→    kv_data["bf16"]  — Tensor (total_kv, 1, 576) bfloat16
    24→    kv_data["fp8"]   — (Tensor, Tensor): kv_buffer fp8 (total_kv,1,576) + scalar scale
    25→    kv_data["mxfp4"] — (Tensor, Tensor): kv_buffer fp4x2 (total_kv,1,288) + fp8_e8m0 scale
    26→  qo_indptr:  (batch_size + 1,)        int32    — query segment pointers
    27→  kv_indptr:  (batch_size + 1,)        int32    — KV segment pointers
    28→  config:     dict with MLA parameters (includes num_heads computed from tp)
    29→
    30→Output:
    31→  attention output: (total_q, num_heads, 512) bfloat16
    32→
    33→The reference uses aiter's a8w8 persistent MLA kernel (fp8 Q + fp8 KV),
    34→which is ~2-3x faster than bf16. To beat it, consider:
    35→  1. Use mxfp4 KV cache for even lower memory bandwidth
    36→     - Fuse dequantization with attention to avoid bf16 materialization
    37→  2. Custom kernel with tighter memory access patterns
    38→  3. MQA: 1 KV head shared across num_heads query heads — minimize redundant memory loads
    39→  4. Variable-length batching: indptr-based segmented attention
    40→  5. Split K/V from buffer: full 576 dims for keys, first 512 dims for values
    41→"""
    42→
    43→import torch
    44→import torch.nn.functional as F
    45→from task import input_t, output_t
    46→
    47→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    48→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    49→
    50→# QKV dtype for custom_kernel dispatch: "bf16", "fp8", or "mxfp4"
    51→QKV_DTYPE = "fp8"
    52→
    53→
    54→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    55→# Dispatcher: select kernel based on QKV_DTYPE
    56→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    57→
    58→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    59→    """Dispatch to the appropriate kernel based on QKV_DTYPE."""
    60→    if QKV_DTYPE == "fp8":
    61→        return custom_kernel_fp8(data)
    62→    elif QKV_DTYPE == "bf16":
    63→        return custom_kernel_bf16(data)
    64→    else:
    65→        raise ValueError(f"Invalid QKV_DTYPE: {QKV_DTYPE}")
    66→
    67→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    68→# FP8 quantization helper (per-tensor, sglang style)
    69→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    70→
    71→def quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    72→    """Dynamic per-tensor FP8 quantization. Returns (fp8_tensor, scale)."""
    73→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    74→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    75→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    76→    fp8_tensor = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)
    77→    return fp8_tensor, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)
    78→
    79→
    80→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    81→# Baseline: bf16 Q + bf16 KV — naive torch attention
    82→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    83→
    84→def custom_kernel_bf16(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    85→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
    86→
    87→    num_heads = config["num_heads"]
    88→    kv_lora_rank = config["kv_lora_rank"]
    89→    sm_scale = config["sm_scale"]
    90→
    91→    # This naive baseline uses bf16 KV directly.
    92→    # For better performance, use kv_data["fp8"] or kv_data["mxfp4"]
    93→    # which are (kv_buffer, kv_scale) tuples. See docstring for optimization opportunities.
    94→    kv_buffer_bf16 = kv_data["bf16"]
    95→
    96→    batch_size = qo_indptr.shape[0] - 1
    97→    out_list = []
    98→
    99→    for i in range(batch_size):
   100→        q_s, q_e = int(qo_indptr[i].item()), int(qo_indptr[i + 1].item())
   101→        kv_s, kv_e = int(kv_indptr[i].item()), int(kv_indptr[i + 1].item())
   102→
   103→        qi = q[q_s:q_e]                       # (seq_q, nhead, 576)
   104→        kvc = kv_buffer_bf16[kv_s:kv_e, 0]    # (seq_kv, 576)  squeeze kv_heads dim
   105→
   106→        # Key: full 576 dims; Value: first 512 dims (kv_lora_rank)
   107→        ki = kvc                       # (seq_kv, 576) — broadcast over heads
   108→        vi = kvc[:, :kv_lora_rank]     # (seq_kv, 512)
   109→
   110→        # Attention: (nhead, seq_q, 576) @ (576, seq_kv) → (nhead, seq_q, seq_kv)
   111→        qi_t = qi.float().permute(1, 0, 2)  # (nhead, seq_q, 576)
   112→        scores = torch.matmul(qi_t * sm_scale, ki.float().T)  # (nhead, seq_q, seq_kv)
   113→
   114→        scores = F.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
   115→
   116→        # Output: (nhead, seq_q, seq_kv) @ (seq_kv, 512) → (nhead, seq_q, 512)
   117→        oi = torch.matmul(scores, vi.float())  # (nhead, seq_q, 512)
   118→        oi = oi.permute(1, 0, 2)               # (seq_q, nhead, 512)
   119→        out_list.append(oi.to(torch.bfloat16))
   120→
   121→    return torch.cat(out_list, dim=0)
   122→
   123→
   124→
   125→
   126→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   127→# FP8 Q + FP8 KV — torch._scaled_mm based attention
   128→#
   129→# Quantize Q to fp8, use fp8 KV from kv_data["fp8"].
   130→# QK^T and softmax@V both use torch._scaled_mm for fp8×fp8 matmul.
   131→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   132→
   133→def custom_kernel_fp8(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   134→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
   135→
   136→    num_heads = config["num_heads"]
   137→    kv_lora_rank = config["kv_lora_rank"]
   138→    qk_head_dim = config["qk_head_dim"]
   139→    sm_scale = config["sm_scale"]
   140→
   141→    # FP8 KV buffer and scale
   142→    kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8 = kv_data["fp8"]
   143→    kv_fp8_2d = kv_buffer_fp8.view(-1, qk_head_dim)    # (total_kv, 576) fp8
   144→
   145→    # Quantize Q to fp8
   146→    q_fp8, q_scale = quantize_fp8(q)                     # q_fp8: (total_q, 16, 576) fp8
   147→
   148→    batch_size = qo_indptr.shape[0] - 1
   149→    out_list = []
   150→
   151→    scale_one = torch.ones(1, dtype=torch.float32, device="cuda")
   152→
   153→    for i in range(batch_size):
   154→        q_s, q_e = int(qo_indptr[i].item()), int(qo_indptr[i + 1].item())
   155→        kv_s, kv_e = int(kv_indptr[i].item()), int(kv_indptr[i + 1].item())
   156→        seq_q = q_e - q_s
   157→        seq_kv = kv_e - kv_s
   158→
   159→        # Q: (seq_q * nhead, 576) fp8,  K: (seq_kv, 576) fp8
   160→        qi_fp8 = q_fp8[q_s:q_e].reshape(seq_q * num_heads, qk_head_dim)   # (seq_q*16, 576)
   161→        ki_fp8 = kv_fp8_2d[kv_s:kv_e]                                      # (seq_kv, 576)
   162→
   163→        # QK^T via _scaled_mm: (seq_q*16, 576) @ (seq_kv, 576).T -> (seq_q*16, seq_kv)
   164→        # _scaled_mm expects (M,K) @ (N,K).T  where b is row-major contiguous
   165→        raw_scores = torch._scaled_mm(
   166→            qi_fp8, ki_fp8.t(),
   167→            scale_a=q_scale, scale_b=kv_scale_fp8,
   168→            out_dtype=torch.float32,
   169→        )
   170→        # raw_scores: (seq_q*16, seq_kv)
   171→        scores = raw_scores.view(seq_q, num_heads, seq_kv).permute(1, 0, 2)  # (nhead, seq_q, seq_kv)
   172→        scores = scores * sm_scale
   173→        scores = F.softmax(scores, dim=-1)
   174→
   175→        # V: first 512 dims of KV buffer (bf16 for softmax@V since scores are float)
   176→        kv_bf16 = kv_data["bf16"]
   177→        vi = kv_bf16[kv_s:kv_e, 0, :kv_lora_rank].float()  # (seq_kv, 512)
   178→
   179→        # softmax @ V: (nhead, seq_q, seq_kv) @ (seq_kv, 512) -> (nhead, seq_q, 512)
   180→        oi = torch.matmul(scores, vi)
   181→        oi = oi.permute(1, 0, 2)                             # (seq_q, nhead, 512)
   182→        out_list.append(oi.to(torch.bfloat16))
   183→
   184→    return torch.cat(out_list, dim=0)
   185→
   186→
   187→
```

     9→def custom_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    10→    """
    11→    Submission template for DeepSeek-R1 MXFP4 MoE kernel.
    12→
    13→    Input data tuple:
    14→        hidden_states:                [M, d_hidden]                           bf16
    15→        gate_up_weight:               [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]    fp4x2  (raw)
    16→        down_weight:                  [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]      fp4x2  (raw)
    17→        gate_up_weight_scale:         [E, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]            e8m0   (raw)
    18→        down_weight_scale:            [E, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]              e8m0   (raw)
    19→        gate_up_weight_shuffled:      [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]    fp4x2  (shuffled)
    20→        down_weight_shuffled:         [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]      fp4x2  (shuffled)
    21→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled:[padded, flat]                          e8m0   (shuffled)
    22→        down_weight_scale_shuffled:   [padded, flat]                          e8m0   (shuffled)
    23→        topk_weights:                 [M, total_top_k]                        float32
    24→        topk_ids:                     [M, total_top_k]                        int32
    25→        config:                       dict
    26→
    27→    Returns:
    28→        output: [M, d_hidden] bf16
    29→    """
    30→    (
    31→        hidden_states,
    32→        gate_up_weight,
    33→        down_weight,
    34→        gate_up_weight_scale,
    35→        down_weight_scale,
    36→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
    37→        down_weight_shuffled,
    38→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
    39→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,
    40→        topk_weights,
    41→        topk_ids,
    42→        config,
    43→    ) = data
    44→
    45→    hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
    46→    intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
    47→
    48→    output = fused_moe(
    49→        hidden_states,
    50→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
    51→        down_weight_shuffled,
    52→        topk_weights,
    53→        topk_ids,
    54→        expert_mask=None,
    55→        activation=ActivationType.Silu,
    56→        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,
    57→        doweight_stage1=False,
    58→        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
    59→        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
    60→        a1_scale=None,
    61→        a2_scale=None,
    62→        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,
    63→        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,
    64→    )
    65→
    66→    return output
    67→

     1→"""
     2→FP4 quant + FP4 GEMM reference: bf16 A, MXFP4 B -> MXFP4 per-1x32 quant A -> gemm_a4w4 -> bf16 C.
     3→Quant logic follows aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.py (get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)).
     4→"""
     5→import torch
     6→from task import input_t, output_t
     7→from utils import make_match_reference
     8→from aiter import QuantType,dtypes
     9→import aiter
    10→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
    11→# K must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)
    12→SCALE_GROUP_SIZE = 32
    13→
    14→def generate_input(m: int, n: int, k: int, seed: int):# -> input_t:
    15→    """
    16→    Generate random bf16 inputs A [m, k], B [n, k] and quantized MXFP4 B, shuffled B and B_scale.
    17→
    18→    Returns:
    19→        Tuple of (A, B), both bf16 on cuda.
    20→    """
    21→    assert k % 64 == 0, "k must be divisible by 64 (scale group 32 and fp4 pack 2)"
    22→    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
    23→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
    24→    A = torch.randn((m, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    25→    B = torch.randn((n, k), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen)
    26→    
    27→    # quantized mxfp4 B
    28→    quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    29→    B_q, B_scale_sh = quant_func(B, shuffle=True)
    30→    
    31→    # shuffle B(weight) to (16,16) tile coalesced
    32→    B_shuffle = shuffle_weight(B_q, layout=(16, 16))
    33→    return (A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh)
    34→
    35→def run_torch_fp4_mm(
    36→    x: torch.Tensor,
    37→    w: torch.Tensor,
    38→    x_scales: torch.Tensor,
    39→    w_scales: torch.Tensor,
    40→    dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bfloat16,
    41→) -> torch.Tensor:
    42→    """
    43→    PyTorch reference: dequant MXFP4 + E8M0 scale -> f32 -> mm -> dtype.
    44→    Same logic as aiter op_tests/test_gemm_a4w4.run_torch.
    45→    x: [m, k//2] fp4 packed, w: [n, k//2] fp4 packed
    46→    x_scales: [m, k//32] E8M0, w_scales: [n, k//32] E8M0
    47→    Returns: [m, n] in dtype
    48→    """
    49→    from aiter.utility import fp4_utils
    50→
    51→    m, _ = x.shape
    52→    n, _ = w.shape
    53→    # fp4 packed -> f32
    54→    x_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(x)
    55→    w_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(w)
    56→    # E8M0 scale: [*, k//32] -> repeat 32 along k -> f32
    57→    x_scales = x_scales[:m].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    58→    x_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(x_scales)
    59→    x_f32 = x_f32 * x_scales_f32
    60→    w_scales = w_scales[:n].repeat_interleave(SCALE_GROUP_SIZE, dim=1)
    61→    w_scales_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(w_scales)
    62→    w_f32 = w_f32 * w_scales_f32
    63→    return torch.mm(x_f32, w_f32.T).to(dtype)[:m, :n]
    64→
    65→
    66→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
    67→    """
    68→    Reference: MXFP4 per-1x32 quant on A and B; both PyTorch ref and gemm_a4w4 are given.
    69→    Returns gemm_a4w4 for check_implementation.
    70→    """
    71→    A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh = data
    72→    A = A.contiguous()
    73→    B = B.contiguous()
    74→    m, k = A.shape
    75→    n, _ = B.shape
    76→    
    77→    # 1) PyTorch impl just for your reference: dequant fp4 + e8m0 -> f32 -> mm -> bf16
    78→    # Per-1x32 MXFP4 quant
    79→    # quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    80→    # quant_func(x, shuffle=False) -> (dtypes.fp4x2, scale); scale layout matches gemm_a4w4
    81→    # A_q, A_scale = quant_func(A, shuffle=False)
    82→    # B_q, B_scale = quant_func(B, shuffle=False)
    83→
    84→    # gemm_a4w4 expects A [M,K/2], B [N,K/2] as dtypes.fp4x2; A_scale/B_scale [*,K/32] E8M0
    85→    # quant_func returns scale as dtypes.fp8_e8m0; gemm_a4w4 accepts E8M0, no view to uint8 needed
    86→    # slice to exact shapes [m,k_scale] / [n,k_scale] (quant may return padded scale)
    87→    
    88→    # k_scale = k // SCALE_GROUP_SIZE
    89→    # A_scale = A_scale[:m, :k_scale].contiguous()
    90→    # B_scale = B_scale[:n, :k_scale].contiguous()
    91→    # out_torch = run_torch_fp4_mm(A_q, B_q, A_scale, B_scale, torch.bfloat16)
    92→
    93→    # 2) aiter.gemm_a4w4 path: needs shuffled B_q and shuffled scales (see test_gemm_a4w4.py:102-105)
    94→    # Per-1x32 MXFP4 quant
    95→    quant_func = aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
    96→    A_q, A_scale_sh = quant_func(A, shuffle=True)
    97→    # to be noted, aiter also has other a4w4 implements using triton, https://github.com/ROCm/aiter/blob/main/aiter/ops/triton/gemm/basic/gemm_afp4wfp4.py
    98→    out_gemm = aiter.gemm_a4w4(
    99→        A_q,
   100→        B_shuffle,
   101→        A_scale_sh,
   102→        B_scale_sh,
   103→        dtype=dtypes.bf16,
   104→        bpreshuffle=True,
   105→    )
   106→    return out_gemm
   107→
   108→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=1e-02, atol=1e-02)

```
     1→"""
     2→Reference implementation for MLA (Multi-head Latent Attention) decode kernel.
     3→
     4→Uses aiter MLA kernels (mla_decode_fwd) as the reference.
     5→DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb MLA: absorbed q (576), compressed kv_buffer (576),
     6→output v_head_dim = kv_lora_rank = 512.
     7→
     8→The input provides:
     9→  q:       (total_q, num_heads, 576) bfloat16 — absorbed query (num_heads = 128 // tp)
    10→  kv_data: dict with KV cache in three formats:
    11→    "bf16":  Tensor  (total_kv, 1, 576)  bfloat16          — highest precision
    12→    "fp8":   (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp8 + scalar scale — per-tensor quantized
    13→    "mxfp4": (Tensor, Tensor)  kv_buffer fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0  — block-32 quantized
    14→  The reference quantizes Q to fp8 on-the-fly inside ref_kernel.
    15→
    16→The reference kernel quantizes Q to fp8 on-the-fly and uses fp8 KV (a8w8 kernel),
    17→which is ~2-3x faster than bf16 on MI355X with negligible accuracy loss.
    18→
    19→Decode only — persistent mode with get_mla_metadata_v1.
    20→"""
    21→
    22→import torch
    23→import torch.nn.functional as F
    24→from task import input_t, output_t
    25→from utils import make_match_reference
    26→
    27→from aiter.mla import mla_decode_fwd
    28→from aiter import dtypes as aiter_dtypes
    29→from aiter import get_mla_metadata_info_v1, get_mla_metadata_v1
    30→from aiter.utility.fp4_utils import (
    31→    dynamic_mxfp4_quant,
    32→    mxfp4_to_f32,
    33→    e8m0_to_f32,
    34→)
    35→
    36→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    37→# DeepSeek R1 latent MQA constants (forward_absorb path)
    38→# https://huggingface.co/deepseek-ai/DeepSeek-R1-0528/blob/main/config.json
    39→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    40→TOTAL_NUM_HEADS = 128
    41→NUM_KV_HEADS = 1
    42→KV_LORA_RANK = 512
    43→QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM = 64
    44→QK_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK + QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM   # 576
    45→V_HEAD_DIM = KV_LORA_RANK                        # 512
    46→SM_SCALE = 1.0 / (QK_HEAD_DIM ** 0.5)
    47→
    48→PAGE_SIZE = 1
    49→NUM_KV_SPLITS = 32
    50→
    51→# FP8 dtype (platform-specific via aiter)
    52→FP8_DTYPE = aiter_dtypes.fp8
    53→
    54→# Query dtype for the reference kernel: "fp8" or "bf16"
    55→Q_DTYPE = "fp8"
    56→
    57→# KV cache dtype for the reference kernel: "fp8" or "bf16"
    58→KV_DTYPE = "fp8"
    59→
    60→
    61→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    62→# FP8 quantization (sglang style: dynamic per-tensor)
    63→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    64→
    65→def quantize_fp8(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    66→    """
    67→    Dynamic per-tensor FP8 quantization (following sglang scaled_fp8_quant).
    68→
    69→    Args:
    70→        tensor: bf16 tensor to quantize
    71→
    72→    Returns:
    73→        (fp8_tensor, scale) where scale is a scalar float32 tensor.
    74→        Dequantize: fp8_tensor.to(bf16) * scale
    75→    """
    76→    finfo = torch.finfo(FP8_DTYPE)
    77→    amax = tensor.abs().amax().clamp(min=1e-12)
    78→    scale = amax / finfo.max
    79→    fp8_tensor = (tensor / scale).clamp(min=finfo.min, max=finfo.max).to(FP8_DTYPE)
    80→    return fp8_tensor, scale.to(torch.float32).reshape(1)
    81→
    82→
    83→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    84→# MXFP4 quantization (aiter native: block-32, fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0 dtypes)
    85→# Uses aiter.utility.fp4_utils.dynamic_mxfp4_quant
    86→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    87→
    88→def quantize_mxfp4(tensor: torch.Tensor) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    89→    """
    90→    MXFP4 block-wise quantization using aiter's dynamic_mxfp4_quant.
    91→
    92→    Block size = 32. Each block gets an E8M0 scale factor.
    93→    Two FP4 E2M1 values are packed per byte.
    94→
    95→    Args:
    96→        tensor: bf16 tensor of shape [B, M, N] (N must be divisible by 32)
    97→
    98→    Returns:
    99→        (fp4_data, scale_e8m0)
   100→        - fp4_data:   shape [B, M, N//2] in aiter_dtypes.fp4x2
   101→        - scale_e8m0: shape [B*M, ceil(N/32)] padded, in aiter_dtypes.fp8_e8m0
   102→    """
   103→    orig_shape = tensor.shape  # (B, M, N)
   104→    B, M, N = orig_shape
   105→
   106→    # dynamic_mxfp4_quant expects 2D: (B*M, N)
   107→    tensor_2d = tensor.reshape(B * M, N)
   108→    fp4_data_2d, scale_e8m0 = dynamic_mxfp4_quant(tensor_2d)
   109→
   110→    # Reshape fp4_data back to 3D: (B, M, N//2)
   111→    fp4_data = fp4_data_2d.view(B, M, N // 2)
   112→
   113→    return fp4_data, scale_e8m0
   114→
   115→
   116→def dequantize_mxfp4(
   117→    fp4_data: torch.Tensor,
   118→    scale_e8m0: torch.Tensor,
   119→    orig_shape: tuple,
   120→    dtype: torch.dtype = torch.bfloat16,
   121→) -> torch.Tensor:
   122→    """
   123→    Dequantize MXFP4 tensor using aiter utilities.
   124→
   125→    Note: dynamic_mxfp4_quant may pad both row and block dimensions in scale_e8m0.
   126→    We trim scales to match the actual data dimensions.
   127→
   128→    Args:
   129→        fp4_data:   packed FP4 data, shape [B, M, N//2] in fp4x2 or uint8
   130→        scale_e8m0: E8M0 block scale factors (possibly padded) in fp8_e8m0
   131→        orig_shape: original (B, M, N) for reshaping
   132→        dtype:      output dtype
   133→
   134→    Returns:
   135→        Dequantized tensor of shape orig_shape.
   136→    """
   137→    B, M, N = orig_shape
   138→    num_rows = B * M
   139→    block_size = 32
   140→    num_blocks = N // block_size  # actual blocks needed (e.g. 576/32 = 18)
   141→
   142→    # Unpack FP4 to float32: mxfp4_to_f32 expects (..., N//2) -> (..., N)
   143→    fp4_data_2d = fp4_data.reshape(num_rows, N // 2)
   144→    float_vals = mxfp4_to_f32(fp4_data_2d)  # (num_rows, N)
   145→
   146→    # Convert E8M0 scales to float32 and trim padded dimensions
   147→    scale_f32 = e8m0_to_f32(scale_e8m0)  # (padded_rows, padded_blocks)
   148→    scale_f32 = scale_f32[:num_rows, :num_blocks]  # (num_rows, num_blocks)
   149→
   150→    # Apply block scales
   151→    float_vals_blocked = float_vals.view(num_rows, num_blocks, block_size)
   152→    scaled = float_vals_blocked * scale_f32.unsqueeze(-1)
   153→
   154→    return scaled.view(B, M, N).to(dtype)
   155→
   156→
   157→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   158→# Persistent mode metadata helpers
   159→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   160→
   161→def _make_mla_decode_metadata(
   162→    batch_size: int,
   163→    max_q_len: int,
   164→    nhead: int,
   165→    nhead_kv: int,
   166→    q_dtype: torch.dtype,
   167→    kv_dtype: torch.dtype,
   168→    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   169→    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   170→    kv_last_page_len: torch.Tensor,
   171→    num_kv_splits: int = NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   172→):
   173→    """Allocate and populate work buffers for persistent mla_decode_fwd."""
   174→    info = get_mla_metadata_info_v1(
   175→        batch_size, max_q_len, nhead, q_dtype, kv_dtype,
   176→        is_sparse=False, fast_mode=False,
   177→        num_kv_splits=num_kv_splits, intra_batch_mode=True,
   178→    )
   179→    work = [torch.empty(s, dtype=t, device="cuda") for s, t in info]
   180→    (work_metadata, work_indptr, work_info_set,
   181→     reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map) = work
   182→
   183→    # Populate the metadata buffers
   184→    get_mla_metadata_v1(
   185→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
   186→        nhead // nhead_kv,   # num_heads_per_head_k
   187→        nhead_kv,            # num_heads_k
   188→        True,                # is_causal
   189→        work_metadata, work_info_set, work_indptr,
   190→        reduce_indptr, reduce_final_map, reduce_partial_map,
   191→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   192→        kv_granularity=max(PAGE_SIZE, 16),
   193→        max_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
   194→        uni_seqlen_qo=max_q_len,
   195→        fast_mode=False,
   196→        max_split_per_batch=num_kv_splits,
   197→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   198→        dtype_q=q_dtype,
   199→        dtype_kv=kv_dtype,
   200→    )
   201→
   202→    return {
   203→        "work_meta_data": work_metadata,
   204→        "work_indptr": work_indptr,
   205→        "work_info_set": work_info_set,
   206→        "reduce_indptr": reduce_indptr,
   207→        "reduce_final_map": reduce_final_map,
   208→        "reduce_partial_map": reduce_partial_map,
   209→    }
   210→
   211→
   212→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   213→# Aiter reference kernel (decode only)
   214→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   215→
   216→def _aiter_mla_decode(
   217→    q: torch.Tensor,
   218→    kv_buffer: torch.Tensor,
   219→    qo_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   220→    kv_indptr: torch.Tensor,
   221→    config: dict,
   222→    q_scale: torch.Tensor | None = None,
   223→    kv_scale: torch.Tensor | None = None,
   224→) -> torch.Tensor:
   225→    """
   226→    MLA decode attention using aiter persistent-mode kernel.
   227→
   228→    Supports multiple Q/KV dtype combinations:
   229→      - Q_DTYPE="fp8":  fp8 Q + fp8 KV (a8w8) — fastest on MI355X
   230→      - Q_DTYPE="bf16": bf16 Q + bf16 KV (a16w16) — highest precision
   231→
   232→    q:          (total_q, num_heads, 576)  fp8 or bf16
   233→    kv_buffer:  (total_kv, 1, 576)         fp8 or bf16
   234→    q_scale:    scalar float32 (required for fp8 Q, None for bf16)
   235→    kv_scale:   scalar float32 (required for fp8 KV, None for bf16)
   236→    """
   237→    batch_size = config["batch_size"]
   238→    nq = config["num_heads"]
   239→    nkv = config["num_kv_heads"]
   240→    dq = config["qk_head_dim"]
   241→    dv = config["v_head_dim"]
   242→    q_seq_len = config["q_seq_len"]
   243→
   244→    total_kv_len = int(kv_indptr[-1].item())
   245→    kv_indices = torch.arange(total_kv_len, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda")
   246→
   247→    # Reshape kv_buffer to 4D for aiter: (total_kv, page_size, nhead_kv, dim)
   248→    kv_buffer_4d = kv_buffer.view(kv_buffer.shape[0], PAGE_SIZE, nkv, kv_buffer.shape[-1])
   249→
   250→    max_q_len = q_seq_len
   251→    kv_last_page_len = (kv_indptr[1:] - kv_indptr[:-1]).to(torch.int32)
   252→
   253→    # Build persistent-mode metadata
   254→    meta = _make_mla_decode_metadata(
   255→        batch_size, max_q_len, nq, nkv,
   256→        q.dtype, kv_buffer.dtype,
   257→        qo_indptr, kv_indptr, kv_last_page_len,
   258→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   259→    )
   260→
   261→    o = torch.empty((q.shape[0], nq, dv), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda")
   262→    mla_decode_fwd(
   263→        q.view(-1, nq, dq),
   264→        kv_buffer_4d,
   265→        o,
   266→        qo_indptr,
   267→        kv_indptr,
   268→        kv_indices,
   269→        kv_last_page_len,
   270→        max_q_len,
   271→        page_size=PAGE_SIZE,
   272→        nhead_kv=nkv,
   273→        sm_scale=SM_SCALE,
   274→        logit_cap=0.0,
   275→        num_kv_splits=NUM_KV_SPLITS,
   276→        q_scale=q_scale,
   277→        kv_scale=kv_scale,
   278→        intra_batch_mode=True,
   279→        **meta,
   280→    )
   281→    return o
   282→
   283→
   284→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   285→# generate_input / ref_kernel / check_implementation
   286→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   287→
   288→def generate_input(batchsize: int, qseqlen: int, kvseqlen: int, tp: int, seed: int) -> input_t:
   289→    """
   290→    Generate absorbed q and compressed kv_buffer for MLA decode.
   291→
   292→    Args:
   293→        tp: tensor parallelism degree (4 or 8). num_heads = TOTAL_NUM_HEADS // tp.
   294→
   295→    Returns all three KV cache formats in kv_data dict:
   296→      kv_data = {
   297→        "bf16":  Tensor               — (total_kv, 1, 576) bfloat16
   298→        "fp8":   (Tensor, Tensor)     — kv_buffer fp8 + scalar scale
   299→        "mxfp4": (Tensor, Tensor)     — kv_buffer fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0 scale
   300→      }
   301→    """
   302→    assert TOTAL_NUM_HEADS % tp == 0, f"TOTAL_NUM_HEADS ({TOTAL_NUM_HEADS}) must be divisible by tp ({tp})"
   303→    num_heads = TOTAL_NUM_HEADS // tp
   304→
   305→    gen = torch.Generator(device="cuda")
   306→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
   307→
   308→    total_q = batchsize * qseqlen
   309→    total_kv = batchsize * kvseqlen
   310→
   311→    # Absorbed query: (total_q, num_heads, 576) bf16
   312→    q = torch.randn(
   313→        (total_q, num_heads, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   314→        dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen,
   315→    ) * 0.02
   316→
   317→    # Compressed KV buffer: (total_kv, 1, 576) bf16 — the source of truth
   318→    kv_buffer_bf16 = torch.randn(
   319→        (total_kv, NUM_KV_HEADS, QK_HEAD_DIM),
   320→        dtype=torch.bfloat16, device="cuda", generator=gen,
   321→    ) * 0.02
   322→
   323→    # Quantize KV to fp8
   324→    kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8 = quantize_fp8(kv_buffer_bf16)
   325→
   326→    # Quantize KV to mxfp4
   327→    kv_buffer_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4 = quantize_mxfp4(kv_buffer_bf16)
   328→
   329→    # All three KV formats: bf16 is a Tensor, fp8/mxfp4 are (Tensor, Tensor) tuples
   330→    kv_data = {
   331→        "bf16": kv_buffer_bf16,
   332→        "fp8": (kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale_fp8),
   333→        "mxfp4": (kv_buffer_mxfp4, kv_scale_mxfp4),
   334→    }
   335→
   336→    qo_indptr = torch.arange(0, batchsize + 1, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda") * qseqlen
   337→    kv_indptr = torch.arange(0, batchsize + 1, dtype=torch.int32, device="cuda") * kvseqlen
   338→
   339→    config = {
   340→        "batch_size": batchsize,
   341→        "num_heads": num_heads,
   342→        "num_kv_heads": NUM_KV_HEADS,
   343→        "qk_head_dim": QK_HEAD_DIM,
   344→        "kv_lora_rank": KV_LORA_RANK,
   345→        "qk_rope_head_dim": QK_ROPE_HEAD_DIM,
   346→        "v_head_dim": V_HEAD_DIM,
   347→        "q_seq_len": qseqlen,
   348→        "kv_seq_len": kvseqlen,
   349→        "sm_scale": SM_SCALE,
   350→    }
   351→
   352→    return (q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config)
   353→
   354→
   355→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   356→    """Reference MLA decode attention. Uses Q_DTYPE and KV_DTYPE to select kernel variant."""
   357→    q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config = data
   358→
   359→    # Resolve Q
   360→    if Q_DTYPE == "fp8":
   361→        q_input, q_scale = quantize_fp8(q)
   362→    else:
   363→        q_input, q_scale = q, None
   364→
   365→    # Resolve KV
   366→    if KV_DTYPE == "fp8":
   367→        kv_buffer_fp8, kv_scale = kv_data["fp8"]
   368→        kv_input = kv_buffer_fp8
   369→    else:
   370→        kv_input, kv_scale = kv_data["bf16"], None
   371→
   372→    return _aiter_mla_decode(
   373→        q_input, kv_input, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config,
   374→        q_scale=q_scale, kv_scale=kv_scale,
   375→    )
   376→
   377→
   378→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=1e-02, atol=1e-02)
   379→
```

     1→from utils import make_match_reference
     2→from task import input_t, output_t
     3→import torch
     4→import torch.nn.functional as F
     5→from typing import Dict, Tuple, Optional
     6→import math
     7→
     8→import aiter
     9→from aiter import ActivationType, QuantType, dtypes
    10→from aiter.fused_moe import fused_moe
    11→from aiter.utility import fp4_utils
    12→from aiter.ops.shuffle import shuffle_weight
    13→
    14→
    15→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    16→# Constants
    17→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    18→MXFP4_BLOCK_SIZE = 32
    19→PAD_ALIGN = 256
    20→
    21→
    22→def _pad_to(x: int, align: int) -> int:
    23→    return (x + align - 1) // align * align
    24→
    25→
    26→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    27→# generate_input: produce all tensors needed by ref_kernel
    28→#
    29→# Models DeepSeek-R1 MoE layer shapes:
    30→#   - d_hidden = 7168
    31→#   - d_expert = moe_intermediate_size (full=2048, or TP-split)
    32→#   - E_total = n_routed_experts + n_shared_experts (257 or 33)
    33→#   - top_k_total = nexpertspertoken + nsharedexperts (8+1=9)
    34→#
    35→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    36→def generate_input(
    37→    dhidden: int,
    38→    dexpert: int,
    39→    nroutedexperts: int,
    40→    nexpertspertoken: int,
    41→    nsharedexperts: int,
    42→    bs: int,
    43→    seed: int,
    44→) -> input_t:
    45→    d_hidden = dhidden
    46→    d_expert = dexpert
    47→    n_routed_experts = nroutedexperts
    48→    n_shared_experts = nsharedexperts
    49→    routed_top_k = nexpertspertoken
    50→    total_top_k = routed_top_k + n_shared_experts   # e.g. 8 + 1 = 9
    51→    E_total = n_routed_experts + n_shared_experts    # e.g. 256 + 1 = 257
    52→    M = bs  # number of tokens
    53→
    54→    # Padded dimensions (AITER MXFP4 requires 256-alignment)
    55→    d_hidden_pad = _pad_to(d_hidden, PAD_ALIGN)
    56→    d_expert_pad = _pad_to(d_expert, PAD_ALIGN)
    57→
    58→    config = {
    59→        "d_hidden": d_hidden,
    60→        "d_expert": d_expert,
    61→        "d_hidden_pad": d_hidden_pad,
    62→        "d_expert_pad": d_expert_pad,
    63→        "n_routed_experts": n_routed_experts,
    64→        "n_shared_experts": n_shared_experts,
    65→        "n_experts_per_token": routed_top_k,
    66→        "total_top_k": total_top_k,
    67→        "bs": M,
    68→    }
    69→
    70→    gen = torch.Generator(device='cuda')
    71→    gen.manual_seed(seed)
    72→
    73→    # ── hidden_states [M, d_hidden] ──
    74→    hidden_states = torch.randn(
    75→        (M, d_hidden), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, generator=gen,
    76→    )
    77→
    78→    # ── Router: softmax top-k (routed experts only) ──
    79→    router_weight = torch.randn(
    80→        (n_routed_experts, d_hidden), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, generator=gen,
    81→    ) / math.sqrt(d_hidden)
    82→    router_logits = F.linear(hidden_states, router_weight)  # [M, n_routed_experts]
    83→    scores = router_logits.softmax(dim=-1)
    84→    routed_weights, routed_ids = torch.topk(
    85→        scores, k=routed_top_k, dim=-1, sorted=False
    86→    )
    87→    routed_weights = routed_weights.to(torch.float32)
    88→    routed_ids = routed_ids.to(torch.int32)
    89→
    90→    # ── Append shared expert(s): always selected, weight = 1.0 ──
    91→    # Shared experts are indexed as n_routed_experts, n_routed_experts+1, ...
    92→    shared_ids = torch.arange(
    93→        n_routed_experts, E_total, device='cuda', dtype=torch.int32
    94→    ).unsqueeze(0).expand(M, -1)                               # [M, n_shared_experts]
    95→    shared_weights = torch.ones(
    96→        (M, n_shared_experts), device='cuda', dtype=torch.float32
    97→    )
    98→
    99→    topk_ids = torch.cat([routed_ids, shared_ids], dim=-1)        # [M, total_top_k]
   100→    topk_weights = torch.cat([routed_weights, shared_weights], dim=-1)  # [M, total_top_k]
   101→
   102→    gate_up_bf16 = torch.randn(
   103→        (E_total, 2 * d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, generator=gen,
   104→    ) / math.sqrt(d_hidden)
   105→    down_bf16 = torch.randn(
   106→        (E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad), device='cuda', dtype=torch.bfloat16, generator=gen,
   107→    ) / math.sqrt(d_expert)
   108→
   109→    torch_quant = aiter.get_torch_quant(QuantType.per_1x32)
   110→    gate_up_weight, gate_up_weight_scale = torch_quant(gate_up_bf16, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)
   111→    down_weight, down_weight_scale = torch_quant(down_bf16, quant_dtype=dtypes.fp4x2)
   112→    gate_up_weight = gate_up_weight.view(E_total, 2 * d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad // 2)
   113→    down_weight = down_weight.view(E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad // 2)
   114→
   115→    gate_up_weight_shuffled = shuffle_weight(gate_up_weight, layout=(16, 16))
   116→    down_weight_shuffled = shuffle_weight(down_weight, layout=(16, 16))
   117→    gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(gate_up_weight_scale)
   118→    down_weight_scale_shuffled = fp4_utils.e8m0_shuffle(down_weight_scale)
   119→
   120→    return (
   121→        hidden_states,                  # [M, d_hidden]                              bf16
   122→        gate_up_weight,                 # [E_total, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2] fp4x2  (raw)
   123→        down_weight,                    # [E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]   fp4x2  (raw)
   124→        gate_up_weight_scale,           # [E_total, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]         e8m0   (raw)
   125→        down_weight_scale,              # [E_total, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]           e8m0   (raw)
   126→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,        # [E_total, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2] fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   127→        down_weight_shuffled,           # [E_total, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]   fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   128→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,  # [padded, flat]                             e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   129→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,     # [padded, flat]                             e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   130→        topk_weights,                   # [M, total_top_k]                           float32
   131→        topk_ids,                       # [M, total_top_k]                           int32
   132→        config,
   133→    )
   134→
   135→
   136→
   137→
   138→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   139→# ref_kernel_pytorch: pure PyTorch implementation (dequant + matmul)
   140→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   141→def _dequant_mxfp4(weight_fp4, scale_e8m0):
   142→    """
   143→    Dequantize MXFP4 weight to float32.
   144→
   145→    weight_fp4:  [N, K//2]  fp4x2  (raw, not shuffled)
   146→    scale_e8m0:  [padded_N, ceil(K/32)] e8m0   (M-dim padded to 256-align by dynamic_mxfp4_quant)
   147→
   148→    Returns: [N, K] float32
   149→    """
   150→    # fp4x2 -> float32 lookup: [N, K]
   151→    w_f32 = fp4_utils.mxfp4_to_f32(weight_fp4)            # [N, K]
   152→    # e8m0 -> float32 power-of-2 scale: [padded_N, scale_K]
   153→    s_f32 = fp4_utils.e8m0_to_f32(scale_e8m0)             # [padded_N, scale_K]
   154→    N, K = w_f32.shape
   155→    # Trim scale rows to match weight rows (scale M-dim is padded to 256)
   156→    s_f32 = s_f32[:N, :]
   157→    # Broadcast scale across block_size=32 columns
   158→    s_f32 = s_f32.repeat_interleave(MXFP4_BLOCK_SIZE, dim=-1)[:, :K]  # [N, K]
   159→    return w_f32 * s_f32
   160→
   161→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   162→# ref_kernel_pytorch: pure PyTorch implementation (dequant + matmul)
   163→# will not run. only for reference
   164→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   165→def ref_kernel_pytorch(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   166→    """
   167→    Pure PyTorch reference: dequantize MXFP4 weights -> bf16 matmul -> SwiGLU -> matmul.
   168→    Uses the raw (un-shuffled) weights.
   169→    """
   170→    (
   171→        hidden_states,             # [M, d_hidden]          bf16
   172→        gate_up_weight,            # [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]  fp4x2
   173→        down_weight,               # [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]    fp4x2
   174→        gate_up_weight_scale,      # [E, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]          e8m0
   175→        down_weight_scale,         # [E, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]            e8m0
   176→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   177→        down_weight_shuffled,
   178→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   179→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   180→        topk_weights,              # [M, top_k]  float32
   181→        topk_ids,                  # [M, top_k]  int32
   182→        config,
   183→    ) = data
   184→
   185→    d_hidden = config["d_hidden"]
   186→    d_expert = config["d_expert"]
   187→    d_hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"]
   188→    d_expert_pad = config["d_expert_pad"]
   189→    M = hidden_states.shape[0]
   190→    top_k = topk_ids.shape[1]
   191→    E = gate_up_weight.shape[0]
   192→
   193→    # Dequantize all expert weights to float32
   194→    # gate_up: [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad] -> trim to [E, 2*d_expert, d_hidden]
   195→    # down:    [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad]    -> trim to [E, d_hidden, d_expert]
   196→    gate_up_dq = torch.stack([
   197→        _dequant_mxfp4(gate_up_weight[e], gate_up_weight_scale[e])
   198→        for e in range(E)
   199→    ])  # [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad]
   200→    gate_up_dq = gate_up_dq[:, :2 * d_expert, :d_hidden].to(torch.bfloat16)
   201→
   202→    down_dq = torch.stack([
   203→        _dequant_mxfp4(down_weight[e], down_weight_scale[e])
   204→        for e in range(E)
   205→    ])  # [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad]
   206→    down_dq = down_dq[:, :d_hidden, :d_expert].to(torch.bfloat16)
   207→
   208→    # Split gate_up -> gate [E, d_expert, d_hidden], up [E, d_expert, d_hidden]
   209→    gate_w, up_w = gate_up_dq.chunk(2, dim=1)  # each [E, d_expert, d_hidden]
   210→
   211→    # Per-token MoE forward
   212→    output = torch.zeros((M, d_hidden), dtype=torch.bfloat16, device=hidden_states.device)
   213→
   214→    for i in range(M):
   215→        x = hidden_states[i]  # [d_hidden]
   216→        for k in range(top_k):
   217→            eid = topk_ids[i, k].item()
   218→            w = topk_weights[i, k].item()
   219→
   220→            # Stage 1: gate_proj + up_proj + SwiGLU
   221→            gate_out = F.silu(x @ gate_w[eid].T)     # [d_expert]
   222→            up_out = x @ up_w[eid].T                  # [d_expert]
   223→            intermediate = gate_out * up_out           # [d_expert]
   224→
   225→            # Stage 2: down_proj
   226→            # down_dq[eid] is [d_hidden, d_expert], .T is [d_expert, d_hidden]
   227→            expert_out = intermediate @ down_dq[eid].T  # [d_hidden]
   228→
   229→            output[i] += w * expert_out
   230→
   231→    return output
   232→
   233→
   234→
   235→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   236→# ref_kernel: calls AITER fused_moe with MXFP4 quantized weights
   237→# ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   238→def ref_kernel(data: input_t) -> output_t:
   239→    """
   240→    Reference implementation using AITER's fused_moe kernel with MXFP4 quantized weights.
   241→
   242→    Input data tuple (E = n_routed_experts + n_shared_experts, total_top_k = routed + shared):
   243→        hidden_states:                [M, d_hidden]                           bf16
   244→        gate_up_weight:               [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]    fp4x2  (raw, before shuffle)
   245→        down_weight:                  [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]      fp4x2  (raw, before shuffle)
   246→        gate_up_weight_scale:         [E, 2*d_expert_pad, scale_K]            e8m0   (raw, before shuffle)
   247→        down_weight_scale:            [E, d_hidden_pad, scale_K]              e8m0   (raw, before shuffle)
   248→        gate_up_weight_shuffled:      [E, 2*d_expert_pad, d_hidden_pad//2]    fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   249→        down_weight_shuffled:         [E, d_hidden_pad, d_expert_pad//2]      fp4x2  (pre-shuffled)
   250→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled:[padded, flat]                          e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   251→        down_weight_scale_shuffled:   [padded, flat]                          e8m0   (pre-shuffled)
   252→        topk_weights:                 [M, total_top_k]                        float32
   253→        topk_ids:                     [M, total_top_k]                        int32
   254→        config:                       dict
   255→
   256→    Returns:
   257→        output: [M, d_hidden] bf16
   258→    """
   259→    (
   260→        hidden_states,
   261→        gate_up_weight,
   262→        down_weight,
   263→        gate_up_weight_scale,
   264→        down_weight_scale,
   265→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   266→        down_weight_shuffled,
   267→        gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   268→        down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   269→        topk_weights,
   270→        topk_ids,
   271→        config,
   272→    ) = data
   273→
   274→    hidden_pad = config["d_hidden_pad"] - config["d_hidden"]
   275→    intermediate_pad = config["d_expert_pad"] - config["d_expert"]
   276→
   277→    output = fused_moe(
   278→        hidden_states,
   279→        gate_up_weight_shuffled,
   280→        down_weight_shuffled,
   281→        topk_weights,
   282→        topk_ids,
   283→        expert_mask=None,
   284→        activation=ActivationType.Silu,
   285→        quant_type=QuantType.per_1x32,  # MXFP4 uses per_1x32 block scaling
   286→        doweight_stage1=False,
   287→        w1_scale=gate_up_weight_scale_shuffled,
   288→        w2_scale=down_weight_scale_shuffled,
   289→        a1_scale=None,
   290→        a2_scale=None,
   291→        hidden_pad=hidden_pad,
   292→        intermediate_pad=intermediate_pad,
   293→    )
   294→
   295→    return output
   296→
   297→
   298→
   299→check_implementation = make_match_reference(ref_kernel, rtol=5e-2, atol=5e-2)
   300→
```

     1→import base64

     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→from typing import Any, Optional
    11→
    12→import torch.cuda
    13→
    14→from utils import set_seed, clear_l2_cache_large as clear_l2_cache
    15→try:
    16→    from task import TestSpec
    17→except ImportError:
    18→    TestSpec = dict

    22→
    23→class PopcornOutput:
    24→    def __init__(self, fd: int):
    25→        self.file = os.fdopen(fd, 'w')
    26→        os.set_inheritable(fd, False)
    27→    
    28→    def __enter__(self):
    29→        return self
    30→    
    31→    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc_val, exc_tb):
    32→        self.file.close()
    33→    
    34→    def print(self, *args, **kwargs):
    35→        print(*args, **kwargs, file=self.file, flush=True)
    36→    
    37→    def log(self, key, value):
    38→        self.print(f"{key}: {value}")
    39→
    40→
    41→@dataclasses.dataclass
    42→class TestCase:
    43→    args: dict
    44→    spec: str
    45→
    46→
    47→def _combine(a: int, b: int) -> int:
    48→    # combine two integers into one:
    49→    # we need this to generate a secret seed based on the test-level seed and
    50→    # the global secret seed.
    51→    # the test-level seeds are public knowledge, and typically relatively small numbers,
    52→    # so we need to make sure they don't provide any useful info for the full seed.
    53→    # This Cantor construction ensures that if the secret seed is a large number,
    54→    # then so is the overall seed.
    55→    return int(a + (a+b)*(a+b+1)//2)
    56→
    57→
    58→def get_test_cases(file_name: str, seed: Optional[int]) -> list[TestCase]:
    59→    try:
    60→        content = Path(file_name).read_text()
    61→    except Exception as E:
    62→        print(f"Could not open test file`{file_name}`: {E}", file=sys.stderr)
    63→        exit(113)
    64→
    65→    tests = []
    66→    lines = content.splitlines()
    67→    match = r"\s*([a-zA-Z_]\w*):\s*([a-zA-Z_]\w*|[+-]?[0-9]+)\s*"
    68→    for line in lines:
    69→        parts = line.split(";")
    70→        case = {}
    71→        for part in parts:
    72→            matched = re.match(match, part)
    73→            if not re.fullmatch(match, part):
    74→                print(f"invalid test case: '{line}': '{part}'", file=sys.stderr)
    75→                exit(113)
    76→            key = matched[1]
    77→            val = matched[2]
    78→            try:
    79→                val = int(val)
    80→            except ValueError:
    81→                if val == "true":
    82→                    val = True
    83→                elif val == "false":
    84→                    val = False
    85→
    86→            case[key] = val
    87→        tests.append(TestCase(spec=line, args=case))
    88→
    89→    if seed is not None:
    90→        for test in tests:
    91→            if "seed" in test.args:
    92→                test.args["seed"] = _combine(test.args["seed"], seed)
    93→
    94→    return tests
    95→
    96→
    97→@dataclasses.dataclass
    98→class Stats:
    99→    runs: int
   100→    mean: float
   101→    std: float
   102→    err: float
   103→    best: float
   104→    worst: float
   105→
   106→
   107→def calculate_stats(durations: list[int]):
   108→    """
   109→    Calculate statistical data from a list of durations.
   110→
   111→    @param durations: A list of durations in nanoseconds.
   112→    @return: A Stats object containing the number of runs, mean, standard deviation, error, best, and worst durations.
   113→    """
   114→    runs = len(durations)
   115→    total = sum(durations)
   116→    best = min(durations)
   117→    worst = max(durations)
   118→
   119→    avg = total / runs
   120→    variance = sum(map(lambda x: (x - avg)**2, durations))
   121→    std = math.sqrt(variance / (runs - 1))
   122→    err = std / math.sqrt(runs)
   123→
   124→    return Stats(runs=runs, mean=avg, std=std, err=err, best=float(best),
   125→                 worst=float(worst))
   126→
   127→
   128→def _clone_data(data):
   129→    """
   130→    Recursively goes through data and clones all tensors.
   131→    """
   132→    if isinstance(data, tuple):
   133→        return tuple(_clone_data(x) for x in data)
   134→    elif isinstance(data, list):
   135→        return [_clone_data(x) for x in data]
   136→    elif isinstance(data, dict):
   137→        return {k: _clone_data(v) for k, v in data.items()}
   138→    elif isinstance(data, torch.Tensor):
   139→        return data.clone()
   140→    else:
   141→        return data
   142→
   143→
   144→def wrap_check_implementation(data, submission_output):
   145→    # Old version returned just a single string, new version
   146→    # returns (bool, str); this function ensures compatibility with old
   147→    # problem definitions.
   148→    result = check_implementation(data, submission_output)
   149→    if isinstance(result, tuple):
   150→        return result
   151→    else:
   152→        return not bool(result), result
   153→
   154→
   155→def _run_single_test(test: TestCase):
   156→    """
   157→    Runs a single test case. Do not call directly
   158→    """
   159→    from submission import custom_kernel
   160→    data = generate_input(**test.args)
   161→    torch.cuda.synchronize()
   162→    submission_output = custom_kernel(_clone_data(data))
   163→    torch.cuda.synchronize()
   164→    return wrap_check_implementation(data, submission_output)
   165→
   166→
   167→def run_single_test(pool: multiprocessing.Pool, test: TestCase):
   168→    """
   169→    Runs a single test in another process.
   170→    """
   171→    return pool.apply(_run_single_test, (test,))
   172→
   173→
   174→def run_testing(logger: PopcornOutput, pool: multiprocessing.Pool, tests: list[TestCase]):
   175→    """
   176→    Executes the actual test case code and checks for correctness.
   177→
   178→    @param logger: A PopcornOutput object used for logging test results.
   179→    @param tests: A list of TestCase objects representing the test cases to be executed.
   180→    @return: An integer representing the exit status: 0 if all tests pass, otherwise 112.
   181→    """
   182→    passed = True
   183→    logger.log("test-count", len(tests))
   184→    for idx, test in enumerate(tests):
   185→        logger.log(f"test.{idx}.spec", test.spec)
   186→        good, message = run_single_test(pool, test)
   187→        if not good:
   188→            logger.log(f"test.{idx}.status", "fail")
   189→            logger.log(f"test.{idx}.error", message)
   190→            passed = False
   191→        else:
   192→            logger.log(f"test.{idx}.status", "pass")
   193→            if message:
   194→                logger.log(f"test.{idx}.message", message)
   195→
   196→    if passed:
   197→        logger.log("check", "pass")
   198→        return 0
   199→    else:
   200→        logger.log("check", "fail")
   201→        return 112
   202→
   203→
   204→def _run_single_benchmark(test: TestCase, recheck: bool, max_repeats: int, max_time_ns: float) -> Stats | Any:
   205→    """
   206→    Runs one benchmark. Do not call directly.
   207→    """
   208→    from submission import custom_kernel
   209→
   210→    durations = []
   211→    # generate input data once
   212→    data = generate_input(**test.args)
   213→    check_copy = _clone_data(data)
   214→    #  first, one obligatory correctness check
   215→    output = custom_kernel(data)
   216→    good, message = wrap_check_implementation(check_copy, output)
   217→    if not good:
   218→        return message
   219→
   220→    # now, do multiple timing runs without further correctness testing
   221→    # there is an upper bound of 100 runs, and a lower bound of 3 runs;
   222→    # otherwise, we repeat until we either measure at least 10 full seconds,
   223→    # or the relative error of the mean is below 1%.
   224→
   225→    bm_start_time = time.perf_counter_ns()
   226→    for i in range(max_repeats):
   227→        if recheck:
   228→            # ensure we use a different seed for every benchmark
   229→            if "seed" in test.args:
   230→                test.args["seed"] += 13
   231→
   232→            data = generate_input(**test.args)
   233→            check_copy = _clone_data(data)
   234→        torch.cuda.synchronize()
   235→        clear_l2_cache()
   236→        start_event = torch.cuda.Event(enable_timing=True)
   237→        end_event = torch.cuda.Event(enable_timing=True)
   238→        start_event.record()
   239→        output = custom_kernel(data)
   240→        end_event.record()
   241→        torch.cuda.synchronize()
   242→
   243→        if recheck:
   244→            good, message = check_implementation(check_copy, output)
   245→            if not good:
   246→                return message
   247→
   248→        del output
   249→        durations.append(start_event.elapsed_time(end_event) * 1e6)
   250→
   251→        if i > 1:
   252→            total_bm_duration = time.perf_counter_ns() - bm_start_time
   253→            stats = calculate_stats(durations)
   254→            # stop if either
   255→            # a) relative error dips below 0.1%
   256→            # b) we exceed the total time limit for benchmarking the kernel
   257→            # c) we exceed 2 minutes of total wallclock time.
   258→            if stats.err / stats.mean < 0.001 or stats.mean * stats.runs > max_time_ns or total_bm_duration > 120e9:
   259→                break
   260→
   261→    return calculate_stats(durations)
   262→
   263→
   264→def run_single_benchmark(pool: multiprocessing.Pool, test: TestCase, recheck: bool, max_repeats: int,
   265→                         max_time_ns: float):
   266→    """
   267→    For a particular test case, check correctness (if applicable) and grab runtime results.
   268→
   269→    @param pool: Process on which the benchmark will be launched.
   270→    @param test: TestCase object.
   271→    @param recheck: Flag for whether to explicitly check functional correctness.
   272→    @param max_repeats: Number of trials to repeat.
   273→    @param max_time_ns: Timeout time in nanoseconds.
   274→    @return: A Stats object for this particular benchmark case or an error if the test fails.
   275→    """
   276→    return pool.apply(_run_single_benchmark, (test, recheck, max_repeats, max_time_ns))
   277→
   278→
   279→def run_benchmarking(logger: PopcornOutput, pool: multiprocessing.Pool, tests: list[TestCase]):
   280→    """
   281→    Executes benchmarking code for a CUDA Kernel and logs runtimes.
   282→
   283→    @param logger: A PopcornOutput object used for logging benchmark results.
   284→    @param pool: Process on which the benchmarks will be launched.
   285→    @param tests: A list of TestCase objects representing the test cases to be benchmarked.
   286→    @return: An integer representing the exit status: 0 if all benchmarks pass, otherwise 112.
   287→    """
   288→    # warm up
   289→    run_single_benchmark(pool, tests[0], False, 100, 10e7)
   290→
   291→    passed = True
   292→    logger.log("benchmark-count", len(tests))
   293→    for idx, test in enumerate(tests):
   294→        logger.log(f"benchmark.{idx}.spec", test.spec)
   295→        result = run_single_benchmark(pool, test, False, 1000, 50e9)
   296→        if isinstance(result, Stats):
   297→            for field in dataclasses.fields(Stats):
   298→                logger.log(f"benchmark.{idx}.{field.name}", getattr(result, field.name))
   299→        else:
   300→            passed = False
   301→            logger.log(f"benchmark.{idx}.status", "fail")
   302→            logger.log(f"benchmark.{idx}.error", result)
   303→
   304→    if passed:
   305→        logger.log("check", "pass")
   306→        return 0
   307→    else:
   308→        logger.log("check", "fail")
   309→        return 112
   310→
   311→
   312→def run_single_profile(test: TestCase) -> str:
   313→    """
   314→    Runs a single test case. Do not call directly
   315→    """
   316→    from submission import custom_kernel
   317→    from torch.profiler import profile, record_function, ProfilerActivity
   318→    data = generate_input(**test.args)
   319→    torch.cuda.synchronize()
   320→
   321→    with profile(activities=[ProfilerActivity.CPU, ProfilerActivity.CUDA]) as prof:
   322→        submission_output = custom_kernel(_clone_data(data))
   323→        torch.cuda.synchronize()
   324→    return prof.key_averages().table(sort_by="self_cuda_time_total", row_limit=20)
   325→
   326→
   327→def run_profiling(logger: PopcornOutput, tests: list[TestCase]):
   328→    logger.log("benchmark-count", len(tests))
   329→    for idx, test in enumerate(tests):
   330→        logger.log(f"benchmark.{idx}.spec", test.spec)
   331→        report = run_single_profile(test)
   332→        logger.log(f"benchmark.{idx}.report", base64.b64encode(report.encode("utf-8"), b"+*").decode("utf-8"))
   333→    logger.log("check", "pass")
   334→    return 0
   335→
   336→
   337→def main():
   338→    fd = os.getenv("POPCORN_FD")
   339→    if not fd:
   340→        return 111
   341→
   342→    if len(sys.argv) < 3:
   343→        return 2
   344→
   345→    mode = sys.argv[1]
   346→    seed = os.getenv("POPCORN_SEED")
   347→    os.unsetenv("POPCORN_SEED")
   348→    seed = int(seed) if seed else None
   349→    set_seed(seed or 42)
   350→    tests = get_test_cases(sys.argv[2], seed)
   351→
   352→    with PopcornOutput(int(fd)) as logger:
   353→        import multiprocessing
   354→        mp_context = multiprocessing.get_context('spawn')
   355→        with mp_context.Pool(1) as pool:
   356→            if mode == "test":
   357→                return run_testing(logger, pool, tests)
   358→            if mode == "benchmark":
   359→                return run_benchmarking(logger, pool, tests)
   360→
   361→            if mode == "leaderboard":
   362→                # warmup
   363→                run_single_benchmark(pool, tests[0], False, 100, 1e7)
   364→                logger.log("benchmark-count", len(tests))
   365→                passed = True
   366→                for i in range(len(tests)):
   367→                    result = run_single_benchmark(pool, tests[i], True, 100, 30e9)
   368→                    logger.log(f"benchmark.{i}.spec", tests[i].spec)
   369→                    if isinstance(result, Stats):
   370→                        for field in dataclasses.fields(Stats):
   371→                            logger.log(f"benchmark.{i}.{field.name}", getattr(result, field.name))
   372→                    else:
   373→                        passed = False
   374→                        logger.log(f"benchmark.{i}.status", "fail")
   375→                        logger.log(f"benchmark.{i}.error", str(result))  # TODO: Make sure result implements __str__?
   376→                        break
   377→
   378→                logger.log("check", "pass" if passed else "fail")
   379→            elif mode == "profile":
   380→                run_profiling(logger, tests)
   381→            else:
   382→                # TODO: Implement script mode
   383→                return 2
   384→
   385→
   386→if __name__ == "__main__":
   387→    sys.exit(main())
   388→

     9→
    10→lang: "py"
    11→
    12→description: |
    13→  You will implement a quantize func and block scaled MXFP4 matrix-matrix multiplication kernel optimized for AMD Instinct MI355X GPU.
    14→  To be explicit, you will be given a tuple of tensors:
    15→  ```
    16→  (A, B, B_q, B_shuffle, B_scale_sh)
    17→  ```
    18→  where:
    19→  * `A` is M x K in K-major order in bfloat16
    20→  * `B` is N x K in K-major order in bfloat16
    21→  * `B_q` is N x K/2 in K-major order in MXFP4
    22→  * `B_shuffle` is N x K/2 in shuffled order in MXFP4, shuffled to (16,16) tile coalesced
    23→  * `B_scale_sh` is * x K/32 in E8M0, * means it will be padded.
    24→
    25→  Then, the kernel flow is bf16 A, MXFP4 B -> MXFP4 per-1x32 quant A -> gemm_a4w4 -> BF16 C [m,n].
    26→  To be specific, the invocation flow is:
    27→  (1) Quant A to MXFP4: aiter.get_triton_quant(QuantType.per_1x32). 
    28→  (2) GEMM: aiter.gemm_a4w4.
    29→  m, n divisible by 64; k divisible by 64.
    30→
    31→  The ranking criteria is the geometric mean of the benchmark results.
    32→  Pls note that this is the elimination round, whoever rank top5 are selected into the next round, e2e optimization for deepseek-R1-MXFP4 and GPTOSS-MXFP4 mdoel
    33→  ```
    34→  The aiter performance is:
    35→  M   N    K   time[us]
    36→    4 2880   512  8.198
    37→   16 2112  7168 20.873
    38→   32 4096   512  9.462
    39→   32 2880   512  9.173
    40→   64 7168  2048 12.738
    41→  256 3072  1536 12.219
    42→  ```

     1→# name: mla-py
     2→
     3→files:
     4→  - {"name": "submission.py", "source": "@SUBMISSION@"}
     5→  - {"name": "task.py", "source": "task.py"}
     6→  - {"name": "utils.py", "source": "../utils.py"}
     7→  - {"name": "reference.py", "source": "reference.py"}
     8→  - {"name": "eval.py", "source": "../eval.py"}
     9→
    10→lang: "py"
    11→
    12→description: |
    13→  Implement a custom MLA (Multi-head Latent Attention) decode kernel optimized for MI355X.
    14→
    15→  This is the inner attention kernel from DeepSeek R1's forward_absorb MLA path.
    16→  The absorbed query and compressed KV cache are provided directly — you only need to
    17→  implement the **attention** computation with variable-length batching (indptr).
    18→
    19→  The reference uses aiter a8w8 MLA decode kernel (mla_decode_fwd, fp8 Q + fp8 KV,
    20→  persistent mode), which is ~2-3x faster than bf16 on MI355X.
    21→
    22→  DeepSeek R1 forward_absorb MLA config:
    23→    - total_num_heads = 128 (query heads before TP split)
    24→    - num_heads = 128 // tp (query heads per device, tp=4 → 32, tp=8 → 16)
    25→    - num_kv_heads = 1 (shared latent KV head)
    26→    - kv_lora_rank = 512
    27→    - qk_rope_head_dim = 64
    28→    - qk_head_dim = 576 (kv_lora_rank + qk_rope_head_dim, absorbed q/k dim)
    29→    - v_head_dim = 512 (= kv_lora_rank, output dim)
    30→    - sm_scale = 1/sqrt(576)
    31→    - dtype: q=bfloat16
    32→    - q_seq_len = 1 or 4, kv_seq_len up to 8k
    33→
    34→  KV buffer format (forward_absorb):
    35→    - Full 576 dims are used as keys (for Q@K^T score computation)
    36→    - First 512 dims (kv_lora_rank) are used as values (for output computation)
    37→
    38→  Input tuple: (q, kv_data, qo_indptr, kv_indptr, config)
    39→    - q: (total_q, num_heads, 576) bfloat16 — absorbed query
    40→    - kv_data: dict with three KV cache formats:
    41→        kv_data["bf16"]  — Tensor (total_kv, 1, 576) bfloat16
    42→        kv_data["fp8"]   — (Tensor, Tensor): kv_buffer fp8 + scalar scale
    43→        kv_data["mxfp4"] — (Tensor, Tensor): kv_buffer fp4x2 + fp8_e8m0 scale
    44→    - qo_indptr: (batch_size+1,) int32 — query segment pointers
    45→    - kv_indptr: (batch_size+1,) int32 — KV segment pointers
    46→    - config: dict with MLA parameters (includes num_heads computed from tp)
    47→
    48→  Return:
    49→    - attention output: (total_q, num_heads, 512) bfloat16
    50→
    51→  Key optimization opportunities:
    52→    1. Use mxfp4 KV cache for even lower memory bandwidth (4x savings over bf16)
    53→       - Fuse dequantization with attention to skip bf16 materialization
    54→    2. Custom kernel with tighter memory access patterns
    55→    3. MQA: 1 KV head shared across num_heads query heads — minimize redundant memory loads
    56→    4. q_seq_len=1 or 4, kv_seq_len up to 8k — memory-bound workload
    57→    5. Variable-length batching: indptr-based segmented attention
    58→    6. Split K/V from buffer: full 576 dims for keys, first 512 dims for values
    59→
    60→  The ranking criteria is the geometric mean of the benchmark results.
    61→
    62→config:
    63→  main: "eval.py"
    64→
    65→templates:
    66→  Python: "submission.py"
    67→
    68→test_timeout: 900
    69→benchmark_timeout: 900
    70→ranked_timeout: 1200
    71→
